LLM supporting:  lubag disease, also known as x - linked dominant spinal and bulbar muscular atrophy ( xspbma ), is a rare genetic disorder characterized by progressive muscle weakness and atrophy, primarily affecting the axial and proximal limb muscles. the disease is caused by mutations in the androgen receptor ( ar ) gene, leading to the production of abnormal androgen receptors that accumulate in the nucleus, inhibiting the function of normal androgen receptors. this results in the gradual degeneration of motor neurons and loss of muscle mass, particularly in the spine, bulbar, and limb muscles. lubag disease is typically inherited in an x - linked dominant pattern, meaning that affected individuals are usually males, and females can be carriers or manifesters with a milder phenotype. the clinical presentation includes gait impairment, proximal muscle weakness, and spasticity, which can lead to significant disability. early diagnosis is crucial for appropriate management and to prevent the development of life - threatening complications such as aspiration pneumonia and autonomic dysfunctions. treatment options are limited and focus on symptomatic management, physical therapy, and, in some cases, testosterone enanthate, which may provide modest benefits in certain patients.
